metrics = {'required_files_empty': [], 'required_files_missing': ['sample_data.pkl']}

    def test_required_files_exist(metrics):
        """Test that all required output files exist and are non-empty."""
        missing = []
        empty = []
        for f in REQUIRED_FILES:
            path = WORKDIR / f
            if not path.exists():
                missing.append(f)
            elif path.stat().st_size == 0:
                empty.append(f)
    
        metrics["required_files_missing"] = missing
        metrics["required_files_empty"] = empty

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DhWoiyujZBnAjtsdjyUorj","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Dataset Conversion Notes\n     2→\n     3→## Overview\n     4→- **Dataset**: Sosa et al. 2025 - \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\"\n     5→- **Date started**: 2026-03-10\n     6→- **Goal**: Convert NWB calcium imaging data to decoder-compatible format\n     7→- **Paper**: Nature Neuroscience, DOI: 10.1038/s41593-025-01985-4\n     8→\n     9→## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n    10→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    11→\n    12→Python environment: numpy 2.3.5, torch 2.6.0+cu124, CUDA available\n    13→\n    14→Directory contents:\n    15→- `paper.pdf` - Reference paper\n    16→- `methods.txt` - Extracted methods from paper\n    17→- `decoder.py` - Decoder module\n    18→- `train_decoder.py` - Decoder training script\n    19→- `code/` - Reference code (Sosa_et_al_2024 repo: README, notebooks, src/reward_relative/, docs)\n    20→- `data/` - NWB data, 11 subjects (m3, m4, m7, m11-m15, m17-m19), 12-14 sessions each, ~152 total sessions\n    21→\n    22→---\n    23→\n    24→## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n    25→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    26→\n    27→### Key Functions Identified\n    28→| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n    29→|----------|------|-------|---------|\n    30→| `get_timeseries_data()` | glmUtils.py | LOADING | Extracts trial-aligned behavioral and neural data from sess |\n    31→| `dff()` | preprocessing.py | PROCESSING | Computes dF/F from raw fluorescence: neuropil subtraction, maximin baseline, gaussian smooth |\n    32→| `get_trial_types()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Returns isreward (binary) and morph (env identity) per trial |\n    33→| `get_reward_zones()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Maps scene name to reward zone [start,stop] coords and labels per trial |\n    34→| `define_trial_subsets()` | behavior.py | CURATION | Splits trials into set0/set1 based on switch trial (trial 30) |\n    35→| `calc_place_cells()` | spatial.py | PROCESSING | Identifies place cells via spatial information shuffle test |\n    36→| `multi_anim_sess()` | utilities.py | LOADING | Loads sess pickles, computes dFF, detects PCs, extracts behavior |\n    37→| `dayData` class | dayData.py | PROCESSING | Multi-animal aggregation with speed thresholding, PC detection |\n    38→| `CircularRegression` | decode.py | PROCESSING | Circular-linear regression decoder for position |\n    39→| `train_vs_test_blocks()` | decode.py | PROCESSING | Cross-validated decoder scoring |\n    40→| `get_omission_trials()` | rewardAnalysis.py | LOADING | Gets omission trial indices |\n    41→| `get_omission_inds()` | rewardAnalysis.py | LOADING | Gets rzone entry index on omission trials |\n    42→\n    43→### Key Processing Parameters (from code)\n    44→- **dFF baseline**: maximin filter with 300 sample (~20 sec) window, per trial\n    45→- **dFF formula**: (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n    46→- **Neuropil subtraction**: F_corrected = F - 0.7 * Fneu\n    47→- **dFF smoothing**: Gaussian sigma=2 samples (~0.129 sec)\n    48→- **Deconvolution**: OASIS (from suite2p), tau=0.7\n    49→- **Speed threshold**: 2 cm/s (used in place cell and decoder analysis)\n    50→- **Place cell p-threshold**: 0.05, 100 shuffles, population shuffle method\n    51→- **Interneuron exclusion**: speed correlation > 0.5\n    52→- **Trial alignment**: trial_start_inds to teleport_inds (start-1 to stop-1 in code, 0-indexed)\n    53→- **Position binning**: 10 cm bins, 45 bins for 450 cm track\n    54→- **Track length**: 0-450 cm\n    55→- **Reward zones**: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] (from reward_zone_dict using X,Y,Z keys)\n    56→- **Switch trial**: trial 30 (0-indexed), change_trial parameter in get_reward_zones\n    57→\n    58→### Key Observation: NWB vs Reference Code Data\n    59→The NWB files contain pre-processed data:\n    60→- `Fluorescence/plane0/data` - raw F (n_timepoints x n_cells)\n    61→- `Neuropil/plane0/data` - neuropil F\n    62→- `Deconvolved/plane0/data` - already deconvolved events\n    63→- `iscell` - suite2p cell classification (2 columns: is_cell, probability)\n    64→- `planeIdx` - plane index for multi-plane recordings (m17, m18)\n    65→- Behavioral timeseries all at imaging frame rate (~15.5 Hz)\n    66→- Scene name embedded in `identifier` field\n    67→\n    68→The NWB files already have the deconvolved events computed. We need to:\n    69→1. Compute dF/F ourselves from F and Fneu (same as reference code)\n    70→2. OR use the pre-computed deconvolved events directly\n    71→3. Filter by iscell\n    72→4. The reference decoder (Fig3) uses deconvolved events, NOT dF/F\n    73→\n    74→### Decoder Analysis from Fig3 notebook\n    75→- Uses `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data()` to get continuous timeseries\n    76→- Neural data: deconvolved events (`sess.timeseries['events']`)\n    77→- Speed threshold: 2 cm/s applied; timepoints with speed < 2 cm/s AND nan licks are excluded\n    78→- Lick sensor error correction: trials with >35% of samples having cumulative lick >2 get licks set to NaN\n    79→- Licks capped at 1 (binary)\n    80→- The decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from neural activity\n    81→- Cross-validation: 10-fold, 90% train, 10% test\n    82→- Uses cell type subsets (rr, track, nonreward_remap) identified via dayData\n    83→\n    84→---\n    85→\n    86→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    87→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    88→\n    89→### Data Structure\n    90→NWB files organized as: `data/sub-{mouse}/sub-{mouse}_ses-{session}_behavior+ophys.nwb`\n    91→\n    92→Each NWB file contains:\n    93→- `processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane{N}/data` - raw fluorescence (n_timepoints x n_ROIs)\n    94→- `processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane{N}/data` - neuropil fluorescence\n    95→- `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane{N}/data` - deconvolved events\n    96→- `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` - (n_ROIs, 2): [is_cell, probability]\n    97→- `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/planeIdx` - plane index per ROI\n    98→- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/` - position, speed, lick, reward_zone, trial number, trial_start, teleport, environment, scanning, autoreward, Reward (separate timestamps)\n    99→- `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/imaging_rate` - frame rate\n   100→- `identifier` - contains scene name (e.g., Env1_LocationB_to_A)\n   101→- `general/subject/subject_id` - mouse ID\n   102→- `general/session_id` - session number\n   103→\n   104→Multi-plane animals (m17, m18): Have plane0 and plane1, imaging_rate=31.015625 Hz (15.5 Hz per plane)\n   105→\n   106→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n   107→| Statistic | Value |\n   108→|-----------|-------|\n   109→| Subjects | 11 (m3, m4, m7, m11-m15, m17-m19) |\n   110→| Sessions / subject | m11: 12, all others: 14 |\n   111→| Total sessions | 152 |\n   112→| ROIs/session (example m11 ses-03) | 349 total, 155 iscell=1 |\n   113→| ROIs/session (example m17 ses-03) | 2619 total (1206+1413 planes), 676 iscell=1 |\n   114→| Trials/session (example m11 ses-03) | 80 |\n   115→| Timepoints/session (example m11 ses-03) | 19818 |\n   116→| Frame rate (single plane) | ~15.5 Hz |\n   117→| Frame rate (multi-plane) | ~31 Hz (15.5 Hz per plane) |\n   118→\n   119→---\n   120→\n   121→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   122→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   123→\n   124→### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   125→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   126→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   127→| Total mice (switch) | 11 | \"n = 11 mice\" |\n   128→| Total mice (fixed) | 3 | \"n = 3 mice\" - NOT in our data |\n   129→| Days of imaging | 14 | \"14 days\" (m11 starts day 3, so 12 sessions) |\n   130→| Trials/session | 80.5 +/- 7.4 | \"mean +/- s.d., 80.5 +/- 7.4 trials across 14 mice\" |\n   131→| Neurons/session range | 155-2172 | \"155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" |\n   132→| Imaging rate | ~15.5 Hz | \"unidirectional scanning at ~15.5 Hz\" |\n   133→| Track length | 450 cm | \"450 cm virtual linear track\" |\n   134→| Reward zone size | 50 cm | \"hidden 50 cm reward zone\" |\n   135→| Zone A | 80-130 cm | \"zone A, 80-130 cm\" |\n   136→| Zone B | 200-250 cm | \"zone B, 200-250 cm\" |\n   137→| Zone C | 320-370 cm | \"zone C, 320-370 cm\" |\n   138→| Reward omission rate | ~15% | \"randomly omitted on ~15% of trials\" |\n   139→| Speed threshold | 2 cm/s | \"excluded activity when moving at <2 cm/s\" |\n   140→| Spatial bins | 45 bins x 10 cm | \"45 bins of 10 cm each\" |\n   141→| Switch trial | 30 | \"set0: 30 trials before switch, set1: after switch\" |\n   142→| Interneurons excluded | 0.42+/-0.85% | \"Pearson corr >0.5 with speed\" |\n   143→| Lick error trials | ~0.65% (~81/12376) | \">30% of samples having cumulative lick >2\" |\n   144→| Teleport zone | ~50 cm + 1-10s jitter | \"gray teleport zone\" |\n   145→\n   146→### Processing Details\n   147→1. **dF/F**: maximin baseline (20s sliding window), per trial, (F-baseline)/|baseline|, Gaussian smooth sigma=2 samples\n   148→2. **Deconvolution**: OASIS algorithm (suite2p), tau=0.7\n   149→3. **Neural data used for decoder**: deconvolved events (not dF/F)\n   150→4. **Speed filtering**: exclude frames with speed < 2 cm/s\n   151→5. **Lick cleaning**: if >35% of samples in trial have cumulative lick >2, set trial licks to NaN; then cap at 1\n   152→6. **Temporal alignment**: all behavioral and neural data at ~15.5 Hz frame rate, already synchronized\n   153→\n   154→### Curation Steps\n   155→\n   156→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   157→1. Suite2p automatic classification + manual curation (stored in iscell)\n   158→2. Interneuron exclusion: Pearson correlation of dF/F with speed > 0.5\n   159→3. For NWB data: iscell column 0 == 1 means cell is included\n   160→\n   161→**Trial curation rules**:\n   162→1. Lick sensor error: trials with >35% of samples having cumulative lick count >2 get licks set to NaN\n   163→2. Only include frames within trial boundaries (trial_start to teleport)\n   164→3. Speed threshold: exclude frames with speed < 2 cm/s\n   165→\n   166→### Decoders Trained (in paper)\n   167→| Decoded variable | Metric | Value |\n   168→|---|---|---|\n   169→| Circular RR position (all PCs) | FDE (fraction decoded error) | 0.10 +/- 0.19 |\n   170→| Circular RR position (RR cells) | Decode score (cosine similarity) | 0.29 +/- 0.11 |\n   171→| Circular RR position (TR cells) | Decode score | 0.32 +/- 0.13 |\n   172→| Circular RR position (non-RR remap) | Decode score | 0.29 +/- 0.11 |\n   173→\n   174→---\n   175→\n   176→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   177→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   178→\n   179→### Discrepancies Found\n   180→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   181→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   182→| Reward zone coords | X=[80,130], Y=[200,250], Z=[320,370] | rzone entry ~200 cm for B_to_A session | A=[80-130], B=[200-250], C=[320-370] | Consistent: code maps A->X, B->Y, C->Z |\n   183→| # subjects | 11 switch mice | 11 subject dirs (m3,m4,m7,m11-m15,m17-m19) | 11 switch + 3 fixed | Data has only switch mice, consistent |\n   184→| # sessions for m11 | Starts day 3 | 12 sessions (ses-03 to ses-14) | \"imaging started on day 3 for m11\" | Consistent |\n   185→| Multi-plane | m17, m18 | m17,m18 have plane0+plane1, rate=31Hz | \"m17 and m18 two planes at ~31Hz\" | Consistent |\n   186→| Lick sensor error threshold | >35% samples with lick>2 in code | N/A | \">30% of samples having cumulative lick >2\" | Code uses 35% (>0.35), paper says 30%. Use code value (0.35) |\n   187→| Frame rate | ~15.5 Hz | m11: 15.5078125 Hz | ~15.5 Hz | Consistent |\n   188→| NWB has deconvolved | N/A | Yes, pre-computed | N/A | Can use directly instead of recomputing dF/F then deconvolving |\n   189→| Change trial | default=30, but checks sess.change_reward_trial | Need to verify from NWB | \"first ten post-switch trials\" with autoreward | Use change_trial=30 by default; need to detect from data for robustness |\n   190→\n   191→### Key Consistency Notes\n   192→- The NWB data already has deconvolved events pre-computed via the reference pipeline (suite2p OASIS). We should use these directly.\n   193→- The iscell flags in NWB correspond to the manual curation from suite2p.\n   194→- Reward zone coordinates match between code (using X,Y,Z labels) and paper (A,B,C zones).\n   195→- The scene name is embedded in the NWB `identifier` field and can be parsed to determine reward zone locations.\n   196→- Multi-plane animals have data already interleaved - the behavioral timeseries length matches the fluorescence length.\n   197→\n   198→---\n   199→\n   200→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   201→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   202→\n   203→### Variable Mapping\n   204→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Notes |\n   205→|---|---|---|---|\n   206→| Deconvolved events (iscell-filtered) | neural | Per-trial, time-aligned, (n_neurons, n_timepoints) | Use pre-computed deconvolved from NWB |\n   207→| Time from trial start | input[0] | (t - trial_start) / imaging_rate, continuous seconds | Time-varying |\n   208→| Environment (morph) | input[1] | 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2, per-trial scalar | From `environment` behavioral TS |\n   209→| Trial number within session | input[2] | 0-indexed, per-trial scalar | From `trial number` behavioral TS |\n   210→| Previous trial outcome | input[3] | 0=omitted, 1=rewarded, per-trial scalar | From reward detection in previous trial |\n   211→| Distance to reward zone | output[0] | Discretized into 7 bins, time-varying | Compute from position and reward zone coords |\n   212→| Absolute position | output[1] | Discretized into 5 equal bins (90cm each), time-varying | From `position` behavioral TS |\n   213→| Speed | output[2] | Discretized into 5 bins, time-varying | From `speed` behavioral TS |\n   214→| Lick | output[3] | Binary 0/1, time-varying | From `lick` behavioral TS, cap at 1 |\n   215→| Reward zone location | output[4] | 0=A, 1=B, 2=C, per-trial | From scene name + trial number |\n   216→| Reward outcome | output[5] | 0=no, 1=yes, per-trial | From Reward timestamps within trial |\n   217→\n   218→### Processing Pipeline\n   219→1. **Load NWB**: Read all behavioral and neural data\n   220→2. **Filter neurons**: Apply iscell filter; exclude interneurons (speed corr > 0.5)\n   221→3. **Compute dF/F**: From F and Fneu using maximin baseline (needed for interneuron detection)\n   222→4. **Identify trials**: From trial_start and teleport indices\n   223→5. **Determine reward zones**: Parse scene from identifier, use get_reward_zones logic\n   224→6. **For each trial**: Extract neural (deconvolved events) and behavioral data between trial_start and teleport\n   225→7. **No speed filtering for conversion** - the decoder task specifies speed as an output, so we keep all timepoints\n   226→8. **Lick cleaning**: Apply the 35% threshold for lick sensor errors, cap at 1\n   227→9. **Time bin**: Each imaging frame (~64.5 ms) is one time bin\n   228→\n   229→### Key Decisions\n   230→1. **Neural data = deconvolved events**: The paper's decoder uses deconvolved events, and NWB has them pre-computed\n   231→2. **No speed thresholding for time bins**: Speed is an output to decode, so we keep all frames. The reference decoder applied speed thresholding, but our task is different.\n   232→3. **Time bin = imaging frame**: ~64.5 ms per frame at ~15.5 Hz. This matches the native sampling rate.\n   233→4. **Trial alignment = trial start**: As specified in Decoder Task section.\n   234→5. **Interneuron exclusion**: Compute dF/F to get speed-neural correlation, exclude cells with corr > 0.5\n   235→6. **Change trial = 30**: Default from code; determines when reward zone switches within a session\n   236→7. **Reward detection**: Check if any reward timestamp falls within trial window\n   237→\n   238→### Output Discretization\n   239→- **Distance to reward zone** (7 bins): < -50, [-50,-10], [-10,0), 0, (0,10], [10,50], > 50 cm\n   240→  - Distance = position - nearest_reward_zone_edge (signed, negative=before, 0=in zone)\n   241→  - Need to compute: min distance to any point in reward zone (0 if inside)\n   242→- **Absolute position** (5 bins): [0,90), [90,180), [180,270), [270,360), [360,450] cm\n   243→- **Speed** (5 bins): <2, [2,10), [10,20), [20,40), >=40 cm/s\n   244→- **Lick** (2 bins): 0=no, 1=yes\n   245→- **Reward zone location** (3 bins): 0=A, 1=B, 2=C\n   246→- **Reward outcome** (2 bins): 0=no, 1=yes\n   247→\n   248→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   249→- [ ] Verify neuron count per session matches iscell sum\n \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\"\n- **Date started**: 2026-03-10\n- **Goal**: Convert NWB calcium imaging data to decoder-compatible format\n- **Paper**: Nature Neuroscience, DOI: 10.1038/s41593-025-01985-4\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nPython environment: numpy 2.3.5, torch 2.6.0+cu124, CUDA available\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `paper.pdf` - Reference paper\n- `methods.txt` - Extracted methods from paper\n- `decoder.py` - Decoder module\n- `train_decoder.py` - Decoder training script\n- `code/` - Reference code (Sosa_et_al_2024 repo: README, notebooks, src/reward_relative/, docs)\n- `data/` - NWB data, 11 subjects (m3, m4, m7, m11-m15, m17-m19), 12-14 sessions each, ~152 total sessions\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `get_timeseries_data()` | glmUtils.py | LOADING | Extracts trial-aligned behavioral and neural data from sess |\n| `dff()` | preprocessing.py | PROCESSING | Computes dF/F from raw fluorescence: neuropil subtraction, maximin baseline, gaussian smooth |\n| `get_trial_types()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Returns isreward (binary) and morph (env identity) per trial |\n| `get_reward_zones()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Maps scene name to reward zone [start,stop] coords and labels per trial |\n| `define_trial_subsets()` | behavior.py | CURATION | Splits trials into set0/set1 based on switch trial (trial 30) |\n| `calc_place_cells()` | spatial.py | PROCESSING | Identifies place cells via spatial information shuffle test |\n| `multi_anim_sess()` | utilities.py | LOADING | Loads sess pickles, computes dFF, detects PCs, extracts behavior |\n| `dayData` class | dayData.py | PROCESSING | Multi-animal aggregation with speed thresholding, PC detection |\n| `CircularRegression` | decode.py | PROCESSING | Circular-linear regression decoder for position |\n| `train_vs_test_blocks()` | decode.py | PROCESSING | Cross-validated decoder scoring |\n| `get_omission_trials()` | rewardAnalysis.py | LOADING | Gets omission trial indices |\n| `get_omission_inds()` | rewardAnalysis.py | LOADING | Gets rzone entry index on omission trials |\n\n### Key Processing Parameters (from code)\n- **dFF baseline**: maximin filter with 300 sample (~20 sec) window, per trial\n- **dFF formula**: (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n- **Neuropil subtraction**: F_corrected = F - 0.7 * Fneu\n- **dFF smoothing**: Gaussian sigma=2 samples (~0.129 sec)\n- **Deconvolution**: OASIS (from suite2p), tau=0.7\n- **Speed threshold**: 2 cm/s (used in place cell and decoder analysis)\n- **Place cell p-threshold**: 0.05, 100 shuffles, population shuffle method\n- **Interneuron exclusion**: speed correlation > 0.5\n- **Trial alignment**: trial_start_inds to teleport_inds (start-1 to stop-1 in code, 0-indexed)\n- **Position binning**: 10 cm bins, 45 bins for 450 cm track\n- **Track length**: 0-450 cm\n- **Reward zones**: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] (from reward_zone_dict using X,Y,Z keys)\n- **Switch trial**: trial 30 (0-indexed), change_trial parameter in get_reward_zones\n\n### Key Observation: NWB vs Reference Code Data\nThe NWB files contain pre-processed data:\n- `Fluorescence/plane0/data` - raw F (n_timepoints x n_cells)\n- `Neuropil/plane0/data` - neuropil F\n- `Deconvolved/plane0/data` - already deconvolved events\n- `iscell` - suite2p cell classification (2 columns: is_cell, probability)\n- `planeIdx` - plane index for multi-plane recordings (m17, m18)\n- Behavioral timeseries all at imaging frame rate (~15.5 Hz)\n- Scene name embedded in `identifier` field\n\nThe NWB files already have the deconvolved events computed. We need to:\n1. Compute dF/F ourselves from F and Fneu (same as reference code)\n2. OR use the pre-computed deconvolved events directly\n3. Filter by iscell\n4. The reference decoder (Fig3) uses deconvolved events, NOT dF/F\n\n### Decoder Analysis from Fig3 notebook\n- Uses `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data()` to get continuous timeseries\n- Neural data: deconvolved events (`sess.timeseries['events']`)\n- Speed threshold: 2 cm/s applied; timepoints with speed < 2 cm/s AND nan licks are excluded\n- Lick sensor error correction: trials with >35% of samples having cumulative lick >2 get licks set to NaN\n- Licks capped at 1 (binary)\n- The decoder predicts circular reward-relative position from neural activity\n- Cross-validation: 10-fold, 90% train, 10% test\n- Uses cell type subsets (rr, track, nonreward_remap) identified via dayData\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\nNWB files organized as: `data/sub-{mouse}/sub-{mouse}_ses-{session}_behavior+ophys.nwb`\n\nEach NWB file contains:\n- `processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane{N}/data` - raw fluorescence (n_timepoints x n_ROIs)\n- `processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane{N}/data` - neuropil fluorescence\n- `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane{N}/data` - deconvolved events\n- `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell` - (n_ROIs, 2): [is_cell, probability]\n- `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/planeIdx` - plane index per ROI\n- `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/` - position, speed, lick, reward_zone, trial number, trial_start, teleport, environment, scanning, autoreward, Reward (separate timestamps)\n- `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/imaging_rate` - frame rate\n- `identifier` - contains scene name (e.g., Env1_LocationB_to_A)\n- `general/subject/subject_id` - mouse ID\n- `general/session_id` - session number\n\nMulti-plane animals (m17, m18): Have plane0 and plane1, imaging_rate=31.015625 Hz (15.5 Hz per plane)\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Subjects | 11 (m3, m4, m7, m11-m15, m17-m19) |\n| Sessions / subject | m11: 12, all others: 14 |\n| Total sessions | 152 |\n| ROIs/session (example m11 ses-03) | 349 total, 155 iscell=1 |\n| ROIs/session (example m17 ses-03) | 2619 total (1206+1413 planes), 676 iscell=1 |\n| Trials/session (example m11 ses-03) | 80 |\n| Timepoints/session (example m11 ses-03) | 19818 |\n| Frame rate (single plane) | ~15.5 Hz |\n| Frame rate (multi-plane) | ~31 Hz (15.5 Hz per plane) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Total mice (switch) | 11 | \"n = 11 mice\" |\n| Total mice (fixed) | 3 | \"n = 3 mice\" - NOT in our data |\n| Days of imaging | 14 | \"14 days\" (m11 starts day 3, so 12 sessions) |\n| Trials/session | 80.5 +/- 7.4 | \"mean +/- s.d., 80.5 +/- 7.4 trials across 14 mice\" |\n| Neurons/session range | 155-2172 | \"155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" |\n| Imaging rate | ~15.5 Hz | \"unidirectional scanning at ~15.5 Hz\" |\n| Track length | 450 cm | \"450 cm virtual linear track\" |\n| Reward zone size | 50 cm | \"hidden 50 cm reward zone\" |\n| Zone A | 80-130 cm | \"zone A, 80-130 cm\" |\n| Zone B | 200-250 cm | \"zone B, 200-250 cm\" |\n| Zone C | 320-370 cm | \"zone C, 320-370 cm\" |\n| Reward omission rate | ~15% | \"randomly omitted on ~15% of trials\" |\n| Speed threshold | 2 cm/s | \"excluded activity when moving at <2 cm/s\" |\n| Spatial bins | 45 bins x 10 cm | \"45 bins of 10 cm each\" |\n| Switch trial | 30 | \"set0: 30 trials before switch, set1: after switch\" |\n| Interneurons excluded | 0.42+/-0.85% | \"Pearson corr >0.5 with speed\" |\n| Lick error trials | ~0.65% (~81/12376) | \">30% of samples having cumulative lick >2\" |\n| Teleport zone | ~50 cm + 1-10s jitter | \"gray teleport zone\" |\n\n### Processing Details\n1. **dF/F**: maximin baseline (20s sliding window), per trial, (F-baseline)/|baseline|, Gaussian smooth sigma=2 samples\n2. **Deconvolution**: OASIS algorithm (suite2p), tau=0.7\n3. **Neural data used for decoder**: deconvolved events (not dF/F)\n4. **Speed filtering**: exclude frames with speed < 2 cm/s\n5. **Lick cleaning**: if >35% of samples in trial have cumulative lick >2, set trial licks to NaN; then cap at 1\n6. **Temporal alignment**: all behavioral and neural data at ~15.5 Hz frame rate, already synchronized\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. Suite2p automatic classification + manual curation (stored in iscell)\n2. Interneuron exclusion: Pearson correlation of dF/F with speed > 0.5\n3. For NWB data: iscell column 0 == 1 means cell is included\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. Lick sensor error: trials with >35% of samples having cumulative lick count >2 get licks set to NaN\n2. Only include frames within trial boundaries (trial_start to teleport)\n3. Speed threshold: exclude frames with speed < 2 cm/s\n\n### Decoders Trained (in paper)\n| Decoded variable | Metric | Value |\n|---|---|---|\n| Circular RR position (all PCs) | FDE (fraction decoded error) | 0.10 +/- 0.19 |\n| Circular RR position (RR cells) | Decode score (cosine similarity) | 0.29 +/- 0.11 |\n| Circular RR position (TR cells) | Decode score | 0.32 +/- 0.13 |\n| Circular RR position (non-RR remap) | Decode score | 0.29 +/- 0.11 |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Reward zone coords | X=[80,130], Y=[200,250], Z=[320,370] | rzone entry ~200 cm for B_to_A session | A=[80-130], B=[200-250], C=[320-370] | Consistent: code maps A->X, B->Y, C->Z |\n| # subjects | 11 switch mice | 11 subject dirs (m3,m4,m7,m11-m15,m17-m19) | 11 switch + 3 fixed | Data has only switch mice, consistent |\n| # sessions for m11 | Starts day 3 | 12 sessions (ses-03 to ses-14) | \"imaging started on day 3 for m11\" | Consistent |\n| Multi-plane | m17, m18 | m17,m18 have plane0+plane1, rate=31Hz | \"m17 and m18 two planes at ~31Hz\" | Consistent |\n| Lick sensor error threshold | >35% samples with lick>2 in code | N/A | \">30% of samples having cumulative lick >2\" | Code uses 35% (>0.35), paper says 30%. Use code value (0.35) |\n| Frame rate | ~15.5 Hz | m11: 15.5078125 Hz | ~15.5 Hz | Consistent |\n| NWB has deconvolved | N/A | Yes, pre-computed | N/A | Can use directly instead of recomputing dF/F then deconvolving |\n| Change trial | default=30, but checks sess.change_reward_trial | Need to verify from NWB | \"first ten post-switch trials\" with autoreward | Use change_trial=30 by default; need to detect from data for robustness |\n\n### Key Consistency Notes\n- The NWB data already has deconvolved events pre-computed via the reference pipeline (suite2p OASIS). We should use these directly.\n- The iscell flags in NWB correspond to the manual curation from suite2p.\n- Reward zone coordinates match between code (using X,Y,Z labels) and paper (A,B,C zones).\n- The scene name is embedded in the NWB `identifier` field and can be parsed to determine reward zone locations.\n- Multi-plane animals have data already interleaved - the behavioral timeseries length matches the fluorescence length.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Deconvolved events (iscell-filtered) | neural | Per-trial, time-aligned, (n_neurons, n_timepoints) | Use pre-computed deconvolved from NWB |\n| Time from trial start | input[0] | (t - trial_start) / imaging_rate, continuous seconds | Time-varying |\n| Environment (morph) | input[1] | 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2, per-trial scalar | From `environment` behavioral TS |\n| Trial number within session | input[2] | 0-indexed, per-trial scalar | From `trial number` behavioral TS |\n| Previous trial outcome | input[3] | 0=omitted, 1=rewarded, per-trial scalar | From reward detection in previous trial |\n| Distance to reward zone | output[0] | Discretized into 7 bins, time-varying | Compute from position and reward zone coords |\n| Absolute position | output[1] | Discretized into 5 equal bins (90cm each), time-varying | From `position` behavioral TS |\n| Speed | output[2] | Discretized into 5 bins, time-varying | From `speed` behavioral TS |\n| Lick | output[3] | Binary 0/1, time-varying | From `lick` behavioral TS, cap at 1 |\n| Reward zone location | output[4] | 0=A, 1=B, 2=C, per-trial | From scene name + trial number |\n| Reward outcome | output[5] | 0=no, 1=yes, per-trial | From Reward timestamps within trial |\n\n### Processing Pipeline\n1. **Load NWB**: Read all behavioral and neural data\n2. **Filter neurons**: Apply iscell filter; exclude interneurons (speed corr > 0.5)\n3. **Compute dF/F**: From F and Fneu using maximin baseline (needed for interneuron detection)\n4. **Identify trials**: From trial_start and teleport indices\n5. **Determine reward zones**: Parse scene from identifier, use get_reward_zones logic\n6. **For each trial**: Extract neural (deconvolved events) and behavioral data between trial_start and teleport\n7. **No speed filtering for conversion** - the decoder task specifies speed as an output, so we keep all timepoints\n8. **Lick cleaning**: Apply the 35% threshold for lick sensor errors, cap at 1\n9. **Time bin**: Each imaging frame (~64.5 ms) is one time bin\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neural data = deconvolved events**: The paper's decoder uses deconvolved events, and NWB has them pre-computed\n2. **No speed thresholding for time bins**: Speed is an output to decode, so we keep all frames. The reference decoder applied speed thresholding, but our task is different.\n3. **Time bin = imaging frame**: ~64.5 ms per frame at ~15.5 Hz. This matches the native sampling rate.\n4. **Trial alignment = trial start**: As specified in Decoder Task section.\n5. **Interneuron exclusion**: Compute dF/F to get speed-neural correlation, exclude cells with corr > 0.5\n6. **Change trial = 30**: Default from code; determines when reward zone switches within a session\n7. **Reward detection**: Check if any reward timestamp falls within trial window\n\n### Output Discretization\n- **Distance to reward zone** (7 bins): < -50, [-50,-10], [-10,0), 0, (0,10], [10,50], > 50 cm\n  - Distance = position - nearest_reward_zone_edge (signed, negative=before, 0=in zone)\n  - Need to compute: min distance to any point in reward zone (0 if inside)\n- **Absolute position** (5 bins): [0,90), [90,180), [180,270), [270,360), [360,450] cm\n- **Speed** (5 bins): <2, [2,10), [10,20), [20,40), >=40 cm/s\n- **Lick** (2 bins): 0=no, 1=yes\n- **Reward zone location** (3 bins): 0=A, 1=B, 2=C\n- **Reward outcome** (2 bins): 0=no, 1=yes\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Verify neuron count per session matches iscell sum\n- [ ] Verify trial count matches reference statistics (~80.5 mean)\n- [ ] Verify position ranges are [0, 450] within trials\n- [ ] Verify reward zone entry positions match expected coordinates\n- [ ] Verify ~15% omission rate\n- [ ] Verify ~85% reward rate\n- [ ] Compare total sessions (152) and subjects (11)\n- [ ] The only processing done is combining cells from multiple planes for multi-plane scans.\n   118→\n   119→ii.\n   120→```python\n   121→deconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\n   122→spike_data = []\n   123→nplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\n   124→for plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n   125→    roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n   126→    iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n   127→    spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n   128→    assert spike_data_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({spike_data_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n   129→    spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\n   130→spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n   131→neural_time_bin_size = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\n   132→...\n   133→assert np.isclose(neural_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Neural time bin size ({neural_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n   134→\n   135→```\n   136→\n   137→iii. The deconvolved data are already processed. The neural data rate is the same for all the recordings, once we account for the multi-plane recordings. \n   138→\n   139→## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n   140→\n   141→i. Neural data is filtered based on the `iscell` variable. \n   142→\n   143→ii. \n   144→```python\n   145→deconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\n   146→spike_data = []\n   147→nplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\n   148→for plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n   149→    roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n   150→    iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n   151→    spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n   152→    assert spike_data_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({spike_data_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n   153→    spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\n   154→```\n   155→\n   156→iii. The paper describes quality metrics, and the number of cells after filtering by `iscell` approximately matches ranges in the paper. \n   157→\n   158→## 2-d. How is the `neural` data temporally binned/resampled?\n   159→\n   160→i. The neural data is not binned or resampled, it is kept at the stored rate. \n   161→\n   162→ii. \n   163→```python\n   164→neural_time_bin_size = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\n   165→...\n   166→assert np.isclose(neural_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Neural time bin size ({neural_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n   167→```\n   168→\n   169→iii. Timestamps are not available for the neural data, just a stored `rate`. This rate is the same for all recordings, once one accounts for the multi-plane recordings\n   170→\n   171→## 2-e. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`? \n   172→\n   173→i. The data are the be aligned with the trial start, which doesn't require any additional processing beyond splitting into trials. \n   174→\n   175→ii. N/A\n   176→\n   177→iii. N/A\n   178→\n   179→## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* derived from? \n   180→\n   181→i. This input is derived from the `timestamps` variable that is available for every behavior time series. These were all the same, so I used the `trial_number` time series. \n   182→\n   183→ii. \n   184→```python\n   185→behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n   186→timestamps = behavior_data['trial number'].timestamps[:] \n   187→for trial in range(ntrials):\n   188→    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   189→    timestamps_curr = timestamps[idx]\n   190→    ...\n   191→    input_curr[0] = timestamps_curr - timestamps_curr[0]\n   192→```\n   193→\n   194→iii. Behavior timestamps were consistent with the constant neural data rate, many choices would have resulted in the same result. \n   195→\n   196→## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds*?\n   197→\n   198→i. The initial timestamp for the trial is subtracted. \n   199→\n   200→ii. \n   201→```python\n   202→behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n   203→timestamps = behavior_data['trial number'].timestamps[:] \n   204→for trial in range(ntrials):\n   205→    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   206→    timestamps_curr = timestamps[idx]\n   207→    ...\n   208→    input_curr[0] = timestamps_curr - timestamps_curr[0]\n   209→```\n   210→\n   211→iii. Common sense\n   212→\n   213→## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* aligned with the neural data? \n   214→\n   215→i. Neural and behavioral data is already temporally aligned in the stored data, nothing needed to be done. A check was made that the number of time points was the same in the neural and behavioral data, and cropped to the shortest if they did not match\n   216→\n   217→ii. N/A\n   218→\n   219→iii. Data rates for the neural and behavior data were the same, and all offsets were 0. \n   220→\n   221→## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Environment type* derived from? \n   222→\n   223→i. `environment` behavior time series\n   224→\n   225→ii.\n   226→```python\n   227→vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:][:T] \n   228→for trial in range(ntrials):\n   229→    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   230→    ...\n   231→    vr_environment_curr = vr_environment[idx].astype(int)\n   232→    input_curr[1] = vr_environment_curr\n   233→```\n   234→\n   235→iii. From inspection, this environment variable is constant in the trial and can only be 0 or 1, which matches the ENV1, ENV2 variable description in the paper. \n   236→\n   237→## 4-b. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number* derived from?\n   246→\n   247→i. Trial number is the within-session trial index, derived from the loop counter over trials. The trial boundaries themselves come from the `trial_start` and `teleport` behavior time series.\n   248→\n   249→ii.\n   250→```python\n   251→for trial in range(ntrials):\n   252→    ...\n   253→    input_curr[2] = trial\n   254→```\n   255→\n   256→iii. The trial number is a sequential index (0, 1, 2, ...) within a session. The stored `trial number` variable in the NWB file was not used because it did not agree with the `trial_start` variable.\n   257→\n   258→## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number*?\n   259→\n   260→i. No processing beyond assigning the loop index. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n   261→\n   262→ii.\n   263→```python\n   264→input_curr[2] = trial\n   265→```\n   266→\n   267→iii. The trial number is simply the sequential index of the trial within the session.\n   268→\n   269→## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n   270→\n   271→i. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward event timestamps are matched to behavior timestamps, and a binary `isreward` array marks timepoints where rewards occurred.\n   272→\n   273→ii.\n   274→```python\n   275→reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\n   276→reward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\n   277→reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n   278→isreward = np.zeros(T)\n   279→isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n   280→```\n   281→\n   282→iii. The `Reward` time series has its own timestamps (not aligned to the behavior sampling rate), so `searchsorted` is used to find the nearest timepoint. An assertion checks that the alignment error is within half a time bin.\n   283→\n   284→## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n   285→\n   286→i. For each trial, check whether any reward event occurred in the *previous* trial's time range. For the first trial, set to 0. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n   287→\n   288→ii.\n   289→```python\n   290→if trial == 0:\n   291→    input_curr[3,:] = 0\n   292→else:\n   293→    prev_reward_idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial-1], trial_end_idx[trial-1], dtype=int)\n   294→    isprevreward = np.any(isreward[prev_reward_idx])\n   295→    input_curr[3,:] = int(isprevreward)\n   296→```\n   297→\n   298→iii. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n   299→\n   300→## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n   301→\n   302→i. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n   303→\n   304→ii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n   305→- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n   306→```python\n   307→behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n   308→info['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\n   309→position = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\n   310→for trial in range(ntrials):\n   311→    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   312→    pos_trial = position[idx]\n   313→    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n   314→    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n   315→        continue\n   316→    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n   317→```\n   318→The function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n   319→```python\n   320→reward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n   321→                    'B': [200, 250],\n   322→                    'C': [320, 370]}\n   323→...\n   324→sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n   325→zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n   326→dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n   327→                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n   328→dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n   329→log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n   330→```\n   331→It also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n   332→```python\n   333→pstay = .9\n   334→log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n   335→np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n   336→```\n   337→\n   338→iii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n   339→\n   340→## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n   341→\n   342→i. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.\n   343→\n   344→ii.\n   345→```python\n   346→def compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n   347→    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n   348→    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n   349→    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n   350→    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n   351→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n   352→    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n   353→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n   354→    return distance_to_reward_zone\n   355→```\n   356→\n   357→iii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`.\n   358→\n   359→## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n   360→\n   361→i. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n   362→\n   363→ii.\n   364→```python\n   365→distance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n   366→...\n   367→output_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n   368→```\n   369→\n   370→iii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n   371→\n   372→## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n   373→\n   374→i. The position data and neural data share the same time indices within each trial, so no additional alignment is needed.\n   375→\n   376→ii.\n   377→```python\n   378→idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   379→spike_data_curr = spike_data[idx]\n   380→position_curr = position[idx]\n   381→```\n   382→\n   383→iii. Both neural and behavioral data use the same time indexing (verified by timestamp assertions earlier in the code), so indexing by the same `idx` array ensures alignment.\n   384→\n   385→## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n   386→\n   387→i. Derived from the `position` behavior time series.\n   388→\n   389→ii.\n   390→```python\n   391→position = behavior_data['position'].data[:][:T]\n   392→assert np.allclose(behavior_data['position'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for position do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   393→...\n   394→position_curr = position[idx]\n   395→```\n   396→\n   397→iii. The `position` variable directly records the animal's position in the VR corridor in cm.\n   398→\n   399→## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n   400→\n   401→i. No processing beyond extracting the per-trial slice and discretizing.\n   402→\n   403→ii.\n   404→```python\n   405→position_curr = position[idx]\n   406→output_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n   407→```\n   408→\n   409→iii. Discretized into 5 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, 50, 150, 250, 350, inf]`, corresponding to 100 cm wide bins spanning the corridor. The position ranged from -50 to 450. \n   414→\n   415→ii.\n   416→```python\n   417→position_bins = [-np.inf,50,150,250,350,np.inf]\n   418→...\n   419→output_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n   420→```\n   421→\n   422→iii. The instructions specify \"5 equal-sized bins\". The corridor spans roughly -50 to 450 cm (from survey), so 100 cm bins are approximately equal-sized.\n   423→\n   424→## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n   425→\n   426→i. Same time indices as the neural data within each trial — no additional alignment needed.\n   427→\n   428→ii. Same indexing as neural: `position_curr = position[idx]`\n   429→\n   430→iii. Verified by timestamp assertions.\n   431→\n   432→## 9-a. See answer 7-a.\n   472→\n   473→## 10-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward zone location*?\n   474→\n   475→i. See answer 7-a. \n   476→\n   477→ii. See answer 7-a.\n   478→\n   479→iii. See answer 7-a. \n   480→\n   481→## 11-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward outcome* derived from?\n   482→\n   483→i. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward events have their own timestamps.\n   484→\n   485→ii.\n   486→```python\n   487→reward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\n   488→reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n   489→isreward = np.zeros(T)\n   490→isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n   491→...\n   492→output_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n   493→```\n   494→\n   495→iii. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward outcome*?\n   498→\n   499→i. Reward event timestamps are mapped to the nearest behavior timepoint using `searchsorted`. For each trial, the output is 1 if any reward event occurred within the trial, 0 otherwise. The value is constant across all timepoints in the trial.\n   500→\n   501→ii.\n   502→```python\n   503→reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n   504→err = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\n   505→assert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2)\n   506→isreward = np.zeros(T)\n   507→isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n   508→...\n   509→output_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n   510→```\n   511→\n   512→iii. The alignment error is checked to be within half a time bin. `np.any` is used because a trial either was rewarded or not — the specific timepoint doesn't matter for the per-trial binary output.\n   513→\n   514→## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n   515→\n   516→i. Several cases are handled:\n   517→- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n   518→- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n   519→- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n   520→- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n   521→\n   522→ii.\n   523→```python\n   524→if T != len(timestamps):\n   525→    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n   526→    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n   527→    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n   528→    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n   529→...\n   530→if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n   531→    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n   532→    istrial[trial] = False\n   533→    continue\n   534→```\n   535→\n   536→iii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n   537→\n   538→## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n   539→\n   540→i. The most time-consuming steps are:\n   541→1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n   542→2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n   543→3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n   544→4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n   545→\n   546→ii. N/A\n   547→\n   548→iii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n   549→\n   550→## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n   551→\n   552→i. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n   553→\n   554→ii. N/A\n   555→\n   556→iii. The per-trial loop is the natural structure given variable-length trials. Vectorizing would require padding or masking.\n   557→\n   558→## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n   559→\n   560→i. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n   561→\n   562→ii. N/A\n   563→\n   564→iii. I don't think these could be run at the same time, as it would require too much RAM. The Viterbi output created during surveying is required to process the data. It was convenient to run the survey before deciding how to handle the conversion of the data. \n   565→\n   566→## 13-d. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n```python\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \ninfo['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    pos_trial = position[idx]\n    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n        continue\n    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n```\nThe function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n...\nsigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n```\nIt also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n```python\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n```\n\niii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-d. The alignment error is checked to be within half a time bin. `np.any` is used because a trial either was rewarded or not — the specific timepoint doesn't matter for the per-trial binary output.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The per-trial loop is the natural structure given variable-length trials. Vectorizing would require padding or masking.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. I don't think these could be run at the same time, as it would require too much RAM. The Viterbi output created during surveying is required to process the data. It was convenient to run the survey before deciding how to handle the conversion of the data. \n\n## 13-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. Discretization bins:\n    43→  - 0:  < -50 cm\n    44→  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n    45→  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    46→  - 3: 0 cm\n    47→  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n    48→  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n    49→  - 6: > +50 cm\n    50→- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n    51→- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n    52→  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n    53→  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n    54→  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n    55→  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n    56→  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n    57→- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    58→- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n    59→- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    60→\n    61→## Target Data Format\n    62→\n    63→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    64→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    65→\n    66→```python\n    67→data = {\n    68→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    71→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    72→            ...\n    73→        ],\n    74→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    75→            ...\n    76→        ],\n    77→        ...\n    78→    ],\n    79→\n    80→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    81→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    82→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    83→            ...\n    84→        ],\n    85→        ...\n    86→    ],\n    87→\n    88→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    89→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    90→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    91→            ...\n    92→        ],\n    93→        ...\n    94→    ],\n    95→\n    96→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    97→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    98→\n    99→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n   100→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n   101→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n   102→    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n   103→\n   104→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n   105→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n   106→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n   107→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n   108→        ...\n   109→    ],\n   110→\n   111→    'metadata': {\n   112→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   113→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   114→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   115→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   116→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   117→        ...\n   118→    }\n   119→}\n   120→```\n   121→\n   122→### Key Format Requirements\n   123→\n   124→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   125→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   126→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   127→\n   128→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   129→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   130→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics such as:\n   131→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   132→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   133→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   134→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   135→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   136→\n   137→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   138→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   139→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   140→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   141→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   142→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   143→\n   144→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   145→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   146→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   147→\n   148→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   149→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   150→\n   151→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   152→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   153→\n   154→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   155→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   156→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   157→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   158→\n   159→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   160→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   161→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   162→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   163→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   164→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   165→\n   166→## Python environment\n   167→\n   168→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   169→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   170→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   171→\n   172→## Decoder Reference\n   173→\n   174→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   175→- Optional arguments:\n   176→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   177→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   178→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   179→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   180→\n   181→### Purpose\n   182→\n   183→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   184→1. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference paper and code and these instructions. During testing, the reference version of `train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \n\n---\n\n## Conversion Workflow\n\n**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Each step has explicit completion criteria. Do not proceed to the next step until the current step is complete. Use the exact step names below when documenting progress in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n**STATUS TRACKING**: At the start of each step, update its status to `IN PROGRESS` in CONVERSION_NOTES.md. When complete, update to `COMPLETE`. This helps you and the user track progress.\n\n---\n\n### Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n\n**Goal**: Set up the working environment and create the notes file.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. **FIRST**: Create `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` with the template structure shown at the end of this document\n2.
